# Review of VTherm Smart Boiler 0.2.2 — 2026-10-04

Release 0.2.2 (manifest version 0.2.2b1) at commit 066ce66
(`066ce66294eaf64f5d17c1e238f567e680bca369`), branch `main`. An independent, read-only review:
nothing in the project was changed. This report and the raw material behind it live in
`research/` (git-ignored); throw-away probes and tool outputs in `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/`.

Raw material: `research/2026-10-04-review-raw/` — the merged problem list (`problems.md`), the
seven requirement matrices (`*-matrix.md`) and the notes of the 19 review tasks (`*-notes.md`).
Tables in this report are copied from those files; nothing here is a finding that is not in them.

Paths are relative to `custom_components/vtherm_smart_boiler/` unless they start with a top-level
folder (`tests/`, `sim/`, `docs/`, `SCOPE.md`, …); the matrices use their own abbreviations, given
at the head of each subsection of section 5. "Open after #n" is item n of "Open after 0.2.2" in
`docs/plan-0.2.2.md`.

Contents: 1. Scope, inputs and method · 2. Summary and overall assessment · 3. Problems ·
4. Problems in the specification · 5. Requirements and the coverage matrix · 6. Missing tests ·
7. Top 5 changes to make first · Appendices A–F (tool results, the control and hand-back state
machine, Versatile Thermostat features and the plugin, untested paths, open questions, refuted
findings).

## 1. Scope, inputs and method

### 1.2 Not read, and why

- The earlier reviews and checks — `docs/review-2026-09-24.md`, `docs/review-2026-09-26.md`,
  `docs/review-2026-09-26-vs-0.2.md`, `research/2026-09-24-code-review/`,
  `research/2026-09-26-session-raw/`, every `research/*check*.md` and `research/*recheck*.md`, and
  `research/2026-10-02-z4-*` — and the session summaries (`session-summary-*.md`): left out so
  that this review is independent of them. The tasks did not read one another's notes either.
- `home-assessment.md`: the author's private assessment, never a source (CLAUDE.md).
- `devenv/local.env` and `devenv/ssh/`: the test Home Assistant's address and access data.
- No Home Assistant instance or device was contacted and nothing was deployed. Nothing of the
  project was changed: the Tools pass found the git tree clean before and after, with no file
  modified outside `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/`, and each task checked its own runs.
- Disclosures the tasks made themselves: PL ran two greps over `research/*.md` globs that include
  excluded check notes (one printed only file names, the other filtered their lines out — nothing
  of them was read or used); SC2's grep over `docs/` returned lines of an excluded earlier review
  (not read further, not used); P21's probes, run without `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`, regenerated
  one git-ignored bytecode file (`core/__pycache__/guards.cpython-314.pyc`) from the unchanged
  source, and one probe run executed `tests/integration/test_control.py` once by accident (it
  passed; nothing was kept); tooling-ci-sim read two package `METADATA` files in the project's own
  uv cache (`.tools/cache/uv/`) to check the `ha-minimum` pin — inside the project, but outside the
  review's list of what may be read.

### 1.3 Method

- **Seven requirement tasks** each built a requirement → code → test matrix: SC1 (`SCOPE.md`
  §1–6), SC2 (`SCOPE.md` §7–12), PL (`PLAN.md`, `docs/plan-0.1.md`, `docs/plan-0.2.md`), P21
  (`docs/plan-0.2.1.md`), P22 (`docs/plan-0.2.2.md` with its details), VT (what the plugin relies
  on in VT 10.4.0, vtherm_api 0.5.0 and SmartPI 0.4.0) and HA (Home Assistant 2026.9, HACS and the
  Integration Quality Scale). One row per requirement; the implementing code read at the cited
  lines; the tests read for what they assert, not by their names; a status per row (5.1). 1,160
  rows in all (section 5).
- **Nineteen review tasks** each read the code through one lens (table below), with probes
  where reading was not enough.
- **A Tools pass** ran the suite as CI does, with coverage per module, mypy and ruff
  (Appendix A).
- **Probes** were throw-away tests and scripts under `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/<task>/`, run
  through `scripts/env.sh`; every problem names the probe that showed it.
- **Verification.** Every finding was sent to an adversarial verifier told to refute it — two
  verifiers for anything rated critical. The verifiers ran probes of their own
  (`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/verify-*`) and corrected severities either way. 242 findings were
  kept and 9 refuted (Appendix F).
- **Merge.** The 242 findings were merged into the problems of sections 3, 4 and 6: duplicates
  across tasks became one problem with the highest justified severity. The merger re-read at
  066ce66 the statements of PB-01 to PB-12, PB-17, PB-21, PB-22, PB-59, SB-01, SB-02, SB-03, SB-05
  and SB-08 (marked "[re-read]"); none gave a reason to raise a severity. Result: 178 problems —
  101 PB (code or tests), 39 SB (specification), 38 TB (missing tests).
- **Known items.** "Open after 0.2.2" (items 1–38) and the decisions recorded there and in
  `SCOPE.md` were taken as known; such an item is reported only where it was found wrong,
  misstated or more severe than stated, and the row says so.

"Findings kept" counts each task's findings as it reported them; several tasks often found the
same problem, so the 242 findings make 178 problems.

### 1.4 Severity scale

- **Critical** — a realistic risk of a cold house without an alarm, a boiler left under the
  plugin's control, a write to the boiler's persistent memory, cycling caused by the plugin,
  overheating a circuit, or a control loop stopping without a safe state.
- **High** — wrong control or monitoring in a realistic case, a double loop or leaked listeners,
  a wrong state after restart or reload, a blocked release, a safety-relevant contradiction in the
  specification.
- **Medium** — an edge case, an untested important path, a quality problem with a real
  regression risk, an applicable Integration Quality Scale Bronze or Silver rule not met.
- **Low** — style, small inconsistencies, documentation.
- Missing tests (TB) carry a priority instead: **high** where a cold house, cycling, a lost
  hand-back or a wrong state could hide; **medium** otherwise.

## 2. Summary and overall assessment

**Overall.** 0.2.2b1 is a carefully specified and heavily tested release, and its safety net holds
up under an independent look. No problem was rated critical after verification. Of the 1,160
requirement rows traced in the seven matrices, 987 are met, 124 are deferred by a plan, 44 are
built but not fully tested, 3 are not built and 2 are built otherwise. What the review found lies
at the edges: one way the plugin can cycle a boiler by itself (a fight with another controller
over a relay), state that can be lost across an internal error or a crash, setpoint paths that
cannot tell when the boiler is not heating, hand-back confirmations that go wrong in particular
set-ups, stored options read without checks, and a specification whose "Open after" list and K4
agenda miss items its own rules say they must hold. In plain terms: the plugin does what its
specification asks in the great majority of cases, and most of the problems found can be fixed
with local changes; four of them should be settled before control runs on a real boiler.

**Counts.** 178 problems: 101 in the code or the tests (PB — critical 0, high 2, medium 23, low
76), 39 in the specification (SB — critical 0, high 2, medium 8, low 29) and 38 missing tests (TB —
high priority 11, medium 27). They merge 242 findings of 26 tasks that survived adversarial
verification; 9 findings were refuted. Found by most tasks (4 each): PB-03, SB-09, SB-17, TB-07,
TB-13.

**What holds** (verified by reading, by probes and by the existing tests):

- Every exit hands the boiler back — switch-off, latch, blocker, lost link, every zone unknown with
  a working thermostat, internal error, unload, reload, Home Assistant's stop — and the hand-back
  debt is stored before the first write; a real cancellation keeps it. The gaps found are on error
  paths (PB-04, PB-05, PB-16).
- The stop fits Home Assistant's shutdown window (a 15-s budget inside the 20-s stage, 3 s per
  write). Reloads and unloads leave exactly one control loop, one shutdown job and no timer behind
  (probed); the leaks found are on failure paths (PB-07, PB-59). Home Assistant's own
  blocking-call detector, turned on in a probe, found nothing.
- The writers call only what each path needs; nothing writes hot water, the modulation cap,
  `SH`/`SW` or any boiler parameter, and persistent or unknown write types are blocked.
- The core's precedence, demand (as VT 10.4.0 computes it), recognition and grace, frost, the
  activation delay, the limits and the guards were checked against `SCOPE.md` rule by rule and
  hold, the exceptions being the problems listed; every VT 10.4.0 key the plugin reads exists
  where it reads it.
- Of 3,131 test cases, 3,128 pass and 3 are known, strict xfails; line-and-branch coverage is
  98.51 %, every module at its floor and `config_flow.py` at 100 %; mypy (strict) and ruff are
  clean; 51 of 56 deliberately broken safety decisions were caught by the tests.
- Every Bronze and Silver rule of Home Assistant's Integration Quality Scale is met, exempt, or
  deferred to a 🔒 step (brand images to R2, the documentation to K1, the code owner to K5),
  except Silver `action-exceptions`, met only in part (HA-21: switching control off does not
  raise when its hand-back fails — PB-39, low).

**Critical:** none. The two closest: PB-01 cycles a boiler, but only while another controller
keeps reverting the relay; PB-22 leaves a cold house without an alarm, but only when a device
changes its setpoint range while it is controlled.

**High — code (PB):**

- **PB-01** (relay path) — a reporting relay that another controller keeps switching back is
  re-commanded every 5 min for good and never stepped aside from: a burner start every 5 min, with
  only the "not confirmed" information alarm.
- **PB-02** (control loop) — when the control switch restores "on" at a start, the control store
  is written "off" until control's first write; a crash or power cut in that window brings control
  back off, and a stand-alone boiler does not heat while the user believes control is on.

**High — specification (SB):**

- **SB-01** — with the comfort correction off by default, setpoint paths have no "the boiler does
  not heat" alarm: a lockout with no fault signal mapped alarms nothing until a room has been below
  5 °C for 2 h.
- **SB-02** — VT's not-yet-started thermostats ("off", `is_ready` false) count as the user's "off"
  after the recognition period, so when VT can start none (its Zigbee or Z-Wave integration down)
  the plugin holds the boiler off (`CH=0`, which also masks a wall thermostat) instead of handing
  back.

**High-priority missing tests (TB):**

- **TB-01** — relay-path frost heating over more than one step and its "frost not warming" alarm
  (a broken alarm passes every test today).
- **TB-02** — an owed hand-back whose own attempt raises at setup or after a failed step.
- **TB-03** — the owed hand-back reported when setup fails or the options cannot be read, and a
  renamed entity of a hand-back-only unit.
- **TB-04** — the entity writer's refusals: no grid point for the lowest water temperature or the
  hand-back value; a non-finite or out-of-range setpoint.
- **TB-05** — the stop running out of time before its hand-back starts.
- **TB-06** — a switch change or a restore queued behind a stop.
- **TB-07** — SmartPI's service raising, its recovery, and an unload with a pause owed.
- **TB-08** — VT's central boiler unticked and control added in the same Home Assistant run.
- **TB-09** — 12 h of the acceptance rig with VT-like TPI pulses and the heating read-back mapped,
  at +8 and −5 °C (it holds today in a probe).
- **TB-10** — an expiring setpoint's own lapse after 61 s of silence, and the fall-back rows at
  59 s (a mutant moving the limit to 150 s passes today).
- **TB-11** — a real climate entity (heat/off) as the relay.

**Where the 23 medium code problems are:** the control unit under errors and restarts (PB-04,
PB-05, PB-13, PB-14, PB-16); hand-back confirmation and what judges it (PB-09, PB-10, PB-11,
PB-12, PB-22); demand and VT (PB-03, PB-08, PB-23); setup and stored options (PB-06, PB-07,
PB-24); the relay's rest-state warning (PB-15); the monitor's statistics and alarms (PB-17 to
PB-21); two tests that depend on the order of timers, so CI can go red at random (PB-25). The 8
medium specification problems (SB-03 to SB-10) concern an ignored heating "on", the timeout
hand-back's release, the declared relay timer's lapse window, a relay that stops taking commands
mid-session, VT's count threshold carried over, J4's starts criterion, the incomplete "Open after"
list, and a failed setup that leaves heating off without a repair issue.

**Recommendation.** Before control runs on a real boiler (K4): fix PB-01 and PB-02 — both local
changes with a clear test — and decide SB-01 and SB-02 (Appendix E, questions 1 and 2). Then work
through the Top 5 (section 7), which also takes in most of the medium problems. Monitor-only use
is touched by none of the high problems. Nothing found blocks publication beyond what the 🔒 steps
already plan (brand images at R2; the README at K1, with the items of SB-26; the repository links
hassfest needs at K5, `docs/plan-0.2.1.md` R1).

## 3. Problems

PB — a problem in the code or a wrong test: 101 in all (critical 0, high 2, medium 23, low 76).
Columns: **tasks** — how many tasks found it, and which; **kind** — code, test, or code + spec;
**where** — file:line; **problem** — what goes wrong, the scenario a real house would see, and the
probe that showed it (probe outputs under `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/`, named per row);
**proposal** — the fix and the test that should pin it. "[re-read]" marks a statement the merger
re-read at 066ce66. Severities are as the verifiers corrected them; where a row touches a known
item of "Open after 0.2.2", it says why it is reported anyway. The tables are copied from
`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/problems.md`.

### 3.1 Critical

None. No finding was rated critical after verification. The merger re-read at 066ce66, for the
merged statements: PB-01 to PB-12, PB-17, PB-21, PB-22, PB-59, SB-01, SB-02, SB-03, SB-05, SB-08
(marked "[re-read]"); none gave a reason to raise a severity. Two are close: PB-01's fight cycles a
boiler but needs another controller reverting the relay; PB-22's effect is a cold house without an
alarm, but its trigger (a device changing its range while controlled) is rare. Everything else
rests on the task notes in `research/2026-10-04-review-raw/` and the probes under
`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/` (named per row).

### 3.2 High (2)

| ID | tasks | kind | severity | where | problem | proposal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PB-01 | 1 — relay-path | code | high | core/relay.py:603-606, 671-691 (`_send` keeps `rewrite_pending` only for REWRITE, l.688); core/relay.py:362-407 (`_classify`: after any resend `confirmed_at` is None, so NOT_CONFIRMED comes before every other row) | A reporting relay that another controller keeps switching back is resent every 300 s for good, never stepped aside from, against answer C ("rewrite once, then step aside") and N (the 4th restart in 24 h steps aside). (a) A REWRITE whose service call fails is retried as a RESEND, which clears `rewrite_pending`. (b) Any resend that the other controller reverts within 120 s leaves `confirmed_at` None, so every later check is NOT_CONFIRMED (resent again), never "another controller" and never a counted restart — with the power-cut state "I don't know" or "last", or the other value equal to the declared power-cut state, this needs no failure at all. House: an old automation switches the boiler relay back within seconds; the plugin re-commands it every 5 min — a burner start every 5 min for as long as it lasts, with only the "not confirmed" information alarm. Verifier probes `verify-relay-path-adv/probe_core.out` (failed rewrite: 15 writes, never blocked; the same run without the failure steps aside at +120 s) and `probe_core_fast_revert.out` (power-cut state unknown: 73 resends, one restart counted). More severe than the task stated (medium). [re-read] | Keep `rewrite_pending` through a RESEND of the same command and judge "rewrite not read back within RELAY_CONFIRM_S" from `rewritten_at`; count a resend the relay has not shown within RELAY_CONFIRM_S while it reports the other value as another restart (answer N) or as another controller, so the 4th within 24 h steps aside (rest state written once). Tests: the rewrite failing once at the service → steps aside at +120 s; a controller reverting within 1 s, power-cut state "I don't know" → steps aside within the day. |
| PB-02 | 1 — control-loop | code | high | control.py:1256-1266 (`async_restore_enabled` saves before `self.enabled = on`); control.py:946-958 (`stored()` writes `self.enabled` once `_restored`) | When the control switch restores the user's "on" at a start, the control store is overwritten with `enabled: false`, and it stays so until control's first write saves again. Probe D (`control-loop/probe-results.txt`): control blocked (VT's central boiler unknown) — store "off" at +0, +10 s, +60 s, +600 s. A crash or power cut in that window (control blocked, latched, or waiting for the boiler link or the read-back after a power cut) brings control back off: decision 3's restore is refused (`_plan_restore` needs the stored wish "on"), and on a stand-alone installation nothing heats while the user believes control is on, with only the near-freezing alarm. The existing test `test_a_first_start_without_a_stored_wish_uses_the_restored_switch` checks the store only after a write. [re-read] | Set `self.enabled = on` before the save (or let `stored()` use the restored wish). Test: Given a stored wish "on" and control blocked, When the switch restores "on", Then the control store says `enabled: true` before any write, and after an emulated crash decision 3's restore runs. |

### 3.3 Medium (23)

| ID | tasks | kind | severity | where | problem | proposal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PB-03 | 4 — SC2, control-core, vt-model, spec-document | code + spec | medium | control.py:1869-1876, 1973-1982 (`_follow_no_zone_known` only for every zone unknown); core/controller.py:548-549; core/demand.py:156-173; tests/integration/test_control.py:8981-9015 (pins `alarm_no_zone_known` off); SCOPE.md:542-554 against docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:2696-2699, 2718 | No configured demand criterion can be judged while the zones are known — count threshold 0 with only power or opening (allowed, as in VT; VT's migration can pre-fill it) and VT stops publishing device power, or an update renames it. Demand is unknown: stand-alone the plugin holds heating off (CH=0) while a room calls with its valve open; with a working thermostat it hands back. Only the information binary sensor "a demand criterion has no data" turns on — no "no zone known" alarm and no repair issue, though SCOPE §7 says both rise at once; plan X3 and the test say "alarm entity only", so the specification contradicts itself. Probes: `sc2/probe_criterion_issue.out` (15 min: idle, CH off, no issue), control-core `probe_criteria.py`. [re-read] | Decide one rule and write it the same in SCOPE §7 and X3; then raise decision 3's repair issue for this end state (its own text naming the criterion; error where heating stops, warning after a hand-back) beside the alarm. ## 4. Problems in the specification

SB — a problem in the specification (`SCOPE.md`, `PLAN.md`, the plans and the details), or where
the code and the specification part ways and the specification must decide: 39 in all (critical 0,
high 2, medium 8, low 29). The problem cell begins with how many tasks found it. Copied from
`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/problems.md`.

Document changes need a shown diff and the user's consent (CLAUDE.md), except an addition to a
plan's "Open after" list, which is committed at once with the diff shown in the step's report.

| ID | severity | where | problem | proposal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SB-01 | high | SCOPE.md:827 (matrix row "Changes no read-back can see"), 955, 287-289; core/controller.py:811-820, 1032-1072; control.py:576-581 (BOILER_NOT_RESPONDING is relay-only), 3755-3792 | Found by 2 tasks (SC2, spec-document). Since the comfort correction is off by default (K4.1, 2026-10-03), setpoint paths have no "the boiler does not heat" warning. With no fault signal mapped (none by default), a lockout, a panel set to summer or CH off, or a gas fault leaves control "heating", the read-back confirming the plugin's value, and nothing alarms until a watched room has been below 5 °C for 2 h ("frost not warming"). SCOPE's matrix row still says such changes are "seen only through the comfort correction at its limit", and the correction never rises while the flame is known off anyway (S-24). The relay path has R12's "no sign the boiler heats" after 30 min; the water path, where flame and flow are mandatory, has none. Probe `sc2/probe_lockout.out`: 6 h, a room 3 K short with its valve at 100 %, flame always off — no control alarm, no issue. [re-read] | Add R12's information alarm and a warning repair issue to setpoint paths: heating commanded, a zone calling, the flame known off (or no flow rise) for about 30 min → "no sign the boiler heats", never a hand-back; correct the matrix row. Test: gateway path, a calling zone, the flame off for 31 min with CH on → the alarm on and control still writing; the flame on → off. |
| SB-02 | high | core/readings.py:163-171 (`is_known`, S-34); core/zone_watch.py:67-78; coordinator.py:1071-1077; tests/core/test_zone_watch.py:230-236; research/2026-09-28-x3-vt-zone-observation.md | Found by 1 task (vt-model). VT 10.4.0 shows every thermostat it has not started as "off" with is_ready false (over_switch and over_climate; over_valve may show "heat"), and keeps it so while any underlying device is unavailable. After the recognition period S-34 reads that as the user's "off" — known, no demand — though the X3 observation says the stand-in must never be taken for the user's "off". When VT can start no thermostat (the Zigbee or Z-Wave integration down after a restart), the plugin holds the boiler off (reason no_demand; on a gateway `CH=0`, which also masks the OpenTherm wall thermostat) instead of decision 3's end state (hand back to a working thermostat; the "no zone known" alarm and issue); only the 30-min zone_unknown alarm while control is on. Probes `vt-model/probe_vt_never_starts.txt` and, with the vendored VT, `verify-vt-model-adv/probe_vt_unavailable_underlying.txt`. [re-read] | K4: count a zone without is_ready true as unknown after the recognition period too, and keep S-34 for a started zone that shows "off"; update the tests that encode the present rule. |
| SB-03 | medium | core/guards.py:326-330 (`off_ignored`), 501 (an ignored target is no longer written); core/loop.py:207-212; control.py:2035-2072, 2208-2214; SCOPE.md:753-758 | Found by 2 tasks (control-core, spec-document). "Ignored from the start" on the heating switch stops every heating write for the session; answer O covers only an ignored "off". (a) A broken heating read-back (frozen at off, or the boiler's CH-active status picked despite the field's warning) makes "on" look ignored; VT's later "off" is then never written and CH stays enabled for the session, with no latch (probe `control-core/probe_on_ignored.py`). (b) Where "on" really is not taken, the error-level "house not heated" issue (Z4-11) rises only where a hand-back stops heating; with a thermostat or "own control resumes" the plugin keeps the boiler with heating off and nothing heats, with a warning only. Not in Open after (#16 is a switch that stops taking "off" later in a session). [re-read] | K4: for a two-valued target stop only the state not taken and keep writing the other (VT's "off"), or block and hand back as answer O does for "off"; raise the Z4-11 issue whenever control keeps the boiler while its "on" is ignored. |
| SB-04 | medium | core/hand_back.py:120-123; control.py:2958-2963; SCOPE.md:178-191, 208-212, 1015; docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:210, 1626, 1683 | Found by 2 tasks (control-core, spec-document). ## 5. Requirements and the coverage matrix

### 5.1 How to read the matrix

Each requirement task took its source sentence by sentence and made one row per requirement
(split where the parts of a statement differ in status), read the implementing code at the cited
lines, and read the tests for what they assert rather than going by their names. The matrices of
5.3–5.9 are copied verbatim from the task files in `research/2026-10-04-review-raw/`; only the
heading levels were changed.

Columns: **ID**; **Source** — where the requirement is stated; **Requirement**; **Code** —
file:line of what implements it; **Tests** — the `file::test` that shows it; **Status**;
**Note** (HA: "Verified / note").

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| met | built, and a test shows it; for a text, document, CI or process item, the artefact read or a check run in the review, named in the row |
| met (exempt) | HA only: the rule's own exception applies; counted as met, with the evidence named |
| partial | built but not fully shown by a test, or only part of it built |
| missing | not built, and no release named |
| contradicted | the code does otherwise than the requirement says |
| deferred | a plan or the specification puts it in a later step or release, named in the row (a 🔒 step, "Open after 0.2.2", 0.3 or later) |

Reading notes:

- Rows overlap between tasks (`SCOPE.md` and the plans state many rules twice, and VT and HA
  rows restate some of both), so the 1,160 rows are not 1,160 distinct requirements.
- A row's status is the task's own; verification and the merge did not edit the matrices. Two
  partial rows rest on findings that were later refuted (Appendix F): P21-120 (OTGW firmware over
  MQTT, monitor only, taking 0 bar as a reading — an accepted limit) and HA-50 (two repair issues
  that only inform — by design). P21-85's note cites a refuted finding too.
- Path abbreviations differ by task and are given at the head of each subsection. "Open after #n"
  is item n of "Open after 0.2.2" in `docs/plan-0.2.2.md`; a 🔒 step is one where work stops for
  the user.
- "Finding …" in a note is the task's own label; the problem it became:

| Task | Finding labels → problems |
|---|---|
| SC1 | F1 → PB-26; F2 → SB-11; F3 → SB-12; F4 → SB-14; F5 → SB-13; F6 → SB-15; F7 → PB-73; F8 → SB-16 |
| SC2 | SC2-F01 → PB-03; SC2-F02 → SB-01; SC2-F03 → SB-13; SC2-F04 → SB-17; SC2-F05 → SB-18; SC2-F06 → TB-13; SC2-F07 → PB-34; SC2-F08 → PB-24; SC2-F09 → TB-17 |
| PL | PL-F1 → TB-14; PL-F2 → PB-33; PL-F3 → SB-21; PL-F4 → SB-22; PL-F5 → SB-23; PL-F6 → TB-18 |
| P21 | P21-F1 → SB-11; P21-F2 refuted; P21-F3 → PB-07; P21-F4 → TB-07; P21-F5 → TB-19; P21-F6 refuted; P21-F7 → TB-02 |

### 5.4 SC2 — SCOPE.md §7–12

Source: `SCOPE.md` §7 (lines 450–1072: the stage table, "Across all stages", missing data, how
control decides, boiler protection, the fixed safeguards, the four classes and their matrix, how
control resumes, decision 7, the options, the defaults table, the fixed-values table, the forecast
roadmap), §8 (1074–1112), §9 (1114–1134), §10 (1136–1165), §11 (1167–1318) and §12 (1320–1331),
sentence by sentence; the "Source" column gives `SCOPE.md`'s lines. The bodies of the tests behind
the safety rows and of every row not "met" were read. Every value of the defaults table was
checked against the code and `tests/test_defaults.py`; every value of the fixed-values table
against the code constants by a probe (148 checks, 147 equal; the one difference is the known Open
after #28). Probes: `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/sc2/` (`probe_fixed_values.py`,
`probe_criterion_issue_test.py`, `probe_lockout_test.py`).

331 rows: 300 met, 6 partial, 0 missing, 1 contradicted, 24 deferred.

Paths: code under `custom_components/vtherm_smart_boiler/`, tests under `tests/`.

### 5.5 PL — PLAN.md, plan 0.1, plan 0.2

Source: `PLAN.md`, `docs/plan-0.1.md` and `docs/plan-0.2.md`, read in full: every step marked
done, the layout sections, the rules that shape the releases, J4's acceptance scenarios as far as
they can run in-process, and the release steps K — each as amended by its own annotations and by
`docs/plan-0.2.1.md` and `docs/plan-0.2.2.md`. A J4 row is "met" only where an in-process
scenario against the simulator shows the behaviour; behaviour shown only with fake entities or in
the core is "partial", and the row says so. The test-HA half of J4 is deferred (J2, J4 🔒). Probe:
`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/PL/probe_return/` (PL-131).

160 rows: 132 met, 10 partial, 0 missing, 0 contradicted, 18 deferred.

Paths: `cc/` = `custom_components/vtherm_smart_boiler/`; `tc/` = `tests/core/`, `ti/` =
`tests/integration/`, `ts/` = `tests/sim/`, `tt/` = `tests/tools/`, `t/` = `tests/`. Tests are
`file::test`; a bracket after a test names the parametrised case meant.

### 5.6 P21 — plan 0.2.1

Source: `docs/plan-0.2.1.md` — the decisions and answers of 2026-09-25, the provisional decisions,
every step marked ✅ (L1–L4, M0–M7, N1–N8, O1–O6, U1–U12, W1–W6, R1, R3–R6) and R2 🔒, the
phases' "Done when", "Done for 0.2.1", and "Open after R6" #1–#8 as `docs/plan-0.2.2.md` took them
over. Where a later recorded decision (plan 0.2.2's decisions 1–16, answers A–O, K4.1–K4.3)
changed a requirement, the row says so ("superseded by …") and is judged against the requirement
as it stands now. Probes: `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/P21/`.

146 rows: 138 met, 4 partial, 0 missing, 0 contradicted, 4 deferred. P21-120 (partial) and the
note of P21-85 rest on P21-F2 and P21-F6, both refuted in verification (Appendix F).

Paths: `cc/` = `custom_components/vtherm_smart_boiler/`, `t/` = `tests/`.

### 5.8 VT — Versatile Thermostat 10.4.0, vtherm_api 0.5.0, SmartPI 0.4.0

Source: the verified facts of `CLAUDE.md` (vtherm_api, VT's central configuration, Auto-TPI,
SmartPI); `SCOPE.md` (principles 4, 9 and 12; §5 zone algorithms and the class-3 migration; §7
demand, decision 3, frost, the activation delay; §9; §11); `PLAN.md`; the plans
(`docs/plan-0.1.md` D1/D4, `docs/plan-0.2.md` H3–H7 and K1, `docs/plan-0.2.1.md`
N2/N3/O6/U3/U11, `docs/plan-0.2.2.md` X3/X4/X5/X7/X8/Z1/Z2 with the details of X3 and X7). Every
interface fact was checked in the sources: `VT:` = `vendor/custom_components/versatile_thermostat/`
(10.4.0), `SPI:` = `vendor/custom_components/vtherm_smartpi/` (0.4.0), `API:` = vtherm_api 0.5.0
in `.venv`. Tests marked "(real VT)" run the vendored VT 10.4.0 in-process; all others use fakes
(`tests/integration/harness.py` `FakeZones`) whose keys were compared with the VT and SmartPI
sources. Probes: `.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/VT/`.

79 rows: 65 met, 6 partial, 0 missing, 0 contradicted, 8 deferred. The task's table of VT
features and what the plugin does with each is Appendix C.

Paths: `cc/` = `custom_components/vtherm_smart_boiler/`; `tc/` = `tests/core/`, `ti/` =
`tests/integration/`, `t/` = `tests/`.

### 5.9 HA — Home Assistant 2026.9, HACS, the Integration Quality Scale

Source, read online on 2026-10-04 (nothing stored): the Integration Quality Scale rule list and
the page of every Bronze and Silver rule and most Gold ones; the developer docs on the manifest,
brand images and the config-flow handler; the developer blog from October 2025 to September 2026;
hassfest's `manifest.py` and `translations.py`; hacs.xyz's publishing pages. Interface facts from
Home Assistant 2026.9.3 in `.venv`. Probes (`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/HA/`): control on with two
reloads and an unload — exactly one plugin shutdown job at a time, none after the unload, no
plugin timer left; Home Assistant's blocking-call detection turned on (with `open`, `listdir` and
`import_module` checked too) over setup, 10 min of control, an analysis, a reload and an unload —
no blocking call, and a positive control was detected; the store writes over 2 h of toggling
demand; the store size with a year of day summaries; the device and entity names; every issue's
placeholders against `en.json`. Not run: the real hassfest and HACS action (no repository until
K5), CI's `ha-minimum` leg.

94 rows: 70 met (14 of them exempt), 4 partial, 2 missing, 0 contradicted, 18 deferred. Every
Integration Quality Scale Bronze and Silver rule (HA-01 to HA-30) is met, exempt, or deferred to
a 🔒 step (brand images to `docs/plan-0.2.1.md` R2, the documentation to K1, the code owner to
K5), except Silver `action-exceptions` (HA-21, partial — PB-39). HA-50 (partial) rests on F-03,
refuted in verification (Appendix F).

### 5.10 Requirements without a full test

Every row whose status is **partial** — 44 of the 1,160 — and after them the 5 rows not built
(**missing**) or built otherwise (**contradicted**). The requirement and the note are copied from
the matrices above; the last column, added for this report, names the problem of sections 3, 4 or
6 that carries the row (or its refutation, Appendix F). Of the 44 partial rows, 8 are J4
scenarios shown with fakes or in the core but not against the simulator (PL-127 to PL-144, all
carried by TB-14).

#### Partial (44)

| ID | Status | Requirement | Note (from the matrix) | Problem |

## 6. Missing tests

TB — a test that should exist: 38 in all. Priority **high** where a cold house, cycling, a lost
hand-back or a wrong state could hide (11), **medium** otherwise (27). Each row names where the
untested code or rule is, who found it (and, where a mutation probe showed it, the mutant that
survives), and the test in Given/When/Then form. Copied from
`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/problems.md`.

## 7. Top 5 changes to make first

1. **Stop the relay fights** — PB-01, SB-05, SB-06, PB-44. Keep the one rewrite through
   resends, count a resend the relay never shows against another controller (answers C and N),
   bound the declared timer's lapse window, and let K4 decide a relay that stops taking commands
   mid-session. Today this is the one path where the plugin can cycle a boiler by itself (a burner
   start every 5 min against an automation).
2. **Make the control unit's wish and hand-back debt hold under errors and restarts** — PB-02,
   PB-04, PB-05, PB-12, PB-13, PB-14, PB-16; tests TB-02, TB-05, TB-06. Save the restored wish
   before anything else; key the error path on the debt (holding or owed) and route it through the
   minute gate and the confirmation; raise the fixable owed issue when a hand-back stays
   unconfirmed; keep the lost-link issue across restarts; fold the old debt at the session's first
   write; notice a control-store write that fails.
3. **Make "nothing heats" visible and settle decision 3's end states** — SB-01, SB-02, PB-03,
   PB-23, SB-03, SB-10; test TB-01. A "no sign the boiler heats" alarm on setpoint paths; the
   "no zone known" alarm and issue when no configured criterion can be judged (one rule in SCOPE
   and X3); VT's not-started placeholder as unknown (K4); refuse or handle count 0 with criteria
   some zones cannot feed; the rule for an ignored "on"; an issue when a failed setup leaves
   heating off.
4. **Confirm hand-backs correctly and freeze what judges them** — PB-10, PB-22, PB-09, PB-11,
   SB-04, PB-26, PB-27; tests TB-04, TB-13. The MQTT gateway with an OpenTherm thermostat; a
   hand-back value never moved onto a range; every key that judges an owed release fixed while it
   is owed; a restore within the current limits; the timeout method's release rule; the lowest
   never above the highest.
5. **Make setup and stored options safe, and give K4 the full list** — PB-06, PB-07, PB-24,
   PB-08, SB-09; tests TB-03, TB-08, TB-25. Wrong shapes and non-finite or out-of-range numbers
   refused with a reason and the owed hand-back reported; platforms unloaded after a late setup
   failure and no registry wipe; the restart latch's untick gap; every remainder in "Open after
   0.2.2" (committed at once under the rules) and K4's rows pointing to the items marked K4.

## Appendix B. Control and hand-back state machine

From the state-machine task (`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/state-machine-notes.md`), which read
`control.py`, `core/loop.py`, `core/controller.py`, `core/guards.py`, `core/hand_back.py`,
`transport/writers.py` and `__init__.py` in full, traced every entry and exit of each state through
the pure core (`decide` → `_stopped` → `_waiting_for_data` → fault, recognition, `_nothing_new` →
`_heating_decision`), `loop_step` and the Home Assistant unit, followed what is stored at once and
what with the delayed save, and pinned the behaviour with probes in the project's own test harness
(`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/state-machine/`). The table is copied as the task wrote it. Its
"finding A … H" labels are the task's own: A → PB-11, B → PB-04, C → PB-12, D → PB-31, E → PB-05,
F → PB-32, G → PB-30, H → PB-09.

### B.1 States

Orthogonal flags of the unit: `enabled` (the user's wish, stored), `_holding` (stored as
`controlling`), `_hand_back_pending` (stored), `_restore_pending`, `latched` (stored),
`session.failed` (stored), `_stopped_by_blocker` (stored as `stopped_heating`). Modes are the
core's `ControlMode`.

| State | Entered when | Left when | What is written | What is stored |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Not configured / monitor only | no control section; or control left the options with nothing owed | an options save adds control (reload) | nothing | `enabled: False`, `last_command: None` written once (`_async_forget_control_session`) |
| Hand-back-only unit | control left the options with a hand-back owed (`_hand_back_unit`, built from `taken_with`) | the debt confirmed or settled by hand (unit stays, only follows SmartPI resumes) | the owed safe hand-back, retried every minute; issue `hand_back_owed` every step | `enabled: False`, `last_command: None`, debt fields, `taken_with` while owed |
| Starting (switch not restored) | unit built at setup; `_restored` False | the switch restores the wish, or 60 s pass (then "off") | an owed hand-back is followed first (not while a restore is pending); nothing decided (relay: a pending restore goes out, R11) | wish kept as stored until restored |
| Switched off (DISABLED) | the user switches off (`async_set_enabled(False)`), or the wish restored "off" | the user switches on | hands back once if `controlling` (`_release`), then `_end_session` (new session: latch, guards (but the one rewrite), writer, last command forgotten) | wish at once (before the step); latch gone; `last_command: None` |
| Blocked (NOT_ALLOWED, PRECONDITION) | any blocker (config, runtime, VT central boiler, monitoring period, integration missing, relay checks, HA starting without a kept command, monitor failing, internal error, heating-off-ignored latch) | the blocker goes — at once, no hysteresis (VT boiler unknown: 600 s grace where known off) | hands back once if controlling; S-10 issue after 60 s where a hand-back stops heating | `stopped_heating` at once with the hand-back |
| Waiting for data (WAITING_DATA) | a stale step short of a loss; gateway read-back without a value before the take (P-21); recognition without a held command; restore waiting for its target | fresh data / read-back value / recognition end | nothing (keep-alives stop: an OTGW override lapses after 60 s) | nothing new |
| Recognising (ZONES_RECOGNITION) | unit start; every zone stops answering at once after all answered (VT reload) | every zone reported since it began, or 600 s — never before HA runs | the command held (this session's, or the restored one) with its keep-alives — the restored setpoint not re-limited without a grid (finding A); frost may still add heat | `last_command` as written (1 K rule) |
| Controlling (HEATING, IDLE, FROST, FALLBACK, BOILER_FAULT) | a heating decision with a command (`controlling` True); a start waiting the activation delay writes nothing when taking afresh | switched off, latch, blocker, lost link, zones unknown with a working thermostat, internal error, stop | setpoint and heating on/off through the guards (change, keep-alive 30 s expiring / 5 min held, resend, the one rewrite); external switch kept on | `controlling: True` at once before the first write attempt; `last_command` at once on a 1 K move or on/off change; the one rewrite at once |
| Writes stopped (blocked, one step) | a guard (or the relay rule) finds another controller after its one rewrite | the next step (latch) | nothing | events → saved at once |
| Handed back, no latch (HANDED_BACK) | lost link (stale 5 min within 10) while controlling; every zone unknown with a working thermostat | link fresh 60 s without a break; a zone answers | the safe hand-back once (then the owed follow) | debt fields; hand-back issue for the lost link |
| Latched (HANDED_BACK + latched) | OUTSIDE_CHANGE; WRITE_IGNORED set to hand back; heating "off" (or the relay's "off") ignored from the start | the user switches off (new session); optional return by itself after an hour of the hand-back state (not for relays) | the whole safe hand-back once (relay: rest state once at a step aside) | `latched`, `latched_by`, `step_aside_seen`, alarms OUTSIDE_CHANGE/CONTROL_ERROR — at once |
| Internal error (NOT_ALLOWED + failed) | any exception in the step | any user change of the switch | `_async_hand_back_now`: whole hand-back if controlling, else the owed debt only (finding B: not when the debt was not yet marked; finding E: every step, not every minute) | `failed` at once, before the hand-back |
| Monitor failing | failed refreshes cover 5 min within 10 (`monitor_lost`) | refreshes good for 60 s | hands back once (blocker), V6 issue; note when control holds the boiler again | — |
| Owed hand-back (orthogonal) | every hand-back attempt marks it before writing; a stored debt or `controlling: True` at start | all targets released or taken by another; a successful write of a new session (folded); settled by hand | retried every minute (what is still owed; lowest first again); a lost two-valued target at once; TIMEOUT never rewritten; held target alarm after 10 s, timeout alarm after 180 s, others after the minute | `hand_back_pending`, `release_from`, `release_baseline`, `taken_by_other`, `taken_with` — at once before each attempt; persistent `hand_back_owed` issue at unload (finding C: no issue while running when the writes succeed but nothing confirms) |
| Holding (orthogonal) | set before every write attempt (and the relay's adopted command) | the hand-back confirmed; settled by hand while not controlling | — | `controlling` at once |
| Restore pending (orthogonal) | `_plan_restore` at `restore()` when every decision-3 condition holds | the first write of it goes through (debt folded), or it gives way (condition fails / recognition ends) | the restored command (as stored) during recognition | — |
| Restart, clean | shutdown job → `async_stop` (hand-back, last command kept) | next start: restore at once where allowed, else the owed hand-back at setup | stop: safe hand-back; start: restored command or owed hand-back | everything above at once; final save at coordinator stop |
| Restart, unclean | crash/power cut | next start: `controlling: True` makes the debt owed; restore where allowed, else hand-back first | as above | as above |
| Reload | options save (meaningful change), rename follow, HA's own reload | setup again | stop's hand-back, then the new unit's restore or owed hand-back | as a clean restart |
| Removal | the user deletes the entry (after unload) | — | nothing more (the unload made the last attempt) | owed → persistent, non-fixable issue; stores removed |

### B.2 Observations of the task (not findings)

- No age limit on a restored last command (decision 3 as specified): after a long outage the old
  command runs through HA's start and the recognition period (≤ 10 min after HA runs).
- Blockers end without hysteresis; a flapping blocker would cycle hand-back/retake (none found
  that flaps by itself; the VT boiler blocker has its 600 s grace).
- `hand_back_owed_after_removal` is persistent and not fixable: the user can only ignore it
  (its text says so).
- The stop's 5 s wait raises a persistent owed issue at every reload/restart for devices whose
  read-back is slower than 5 s (R6 by design; noisy).
- `async_create_fix_flow` matches `hand_back_owed_` as a prefix, which also matches
  `hand_back_owed_after_removal_*`; harmless as that issue is not fixable.

## Appendix C. Versatile Thermostat features and the plugin

From the VT task (`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/VT-matrix.md`): the features of VT 10.4.0, what
VT does with each, and what the plugin does with it — where given, with the matrix rows (VT-nn,
section 5.8) that check it. Every key the plugin reads was checked in the vendored sources (VT
10.4.0, SmartPI 0.4.0, vtherm_api 0.5.0). Copied as the task wrote it, with the task's lists of
what it found sound and what it did not check.

### C.1 Features

| VT 10.4.0 feature | What VT does | What the plugin does with it |
|---|---|---|
| Thermostat types (over_switch, over_valve, over_climate, over_climate with valve regulation) | Drives switches, valves or climates per room | Reads any type; demand from VT's device state, else valve opening or duty cycle, else the heating action (VT-34); over_climate without `on_percent` by its action |
| HVAC modes (heat, off, cool; auto/heat_cool on over_climate; sleep shown as "off") | The room's mode | heat may heat; auto/heat_cool by action; off/cool/dry/fan_only no demand; unknown stays unknown (VT-17); a sleeping zone with its valve open can still take frost heat (VT-45) |
| Presets (frost, eco, comfort, boost, away), central presets | Set the target temperature | Read only through the target; the closed-cold-zone issue recommends VT's frost preset instead of "off" |
| TPI (coef_int, coef_ext, thresholds, minimal activation/deactivation, cycle) | Duty cycle per room | Followed through VT's device state (minimal activation honoured); the advice to keep `tpi_coef_ext` small sits in the advanced comfort-correction text (VT-73); the comfort correction is off by default because of TPI's offset (Open after #24) |
| Auto-TPI (learning sessions, continuous Kext) | Learns TPI coefficients; skips learning while VT's central boiler is off or power shedding runs | Detected from `specific_states`; never paused (`set_auto_tpi_mode` not called); `auto_tpi_blocked` for zones flagged as used by the central boiler; `learning_not_paused` while control could not protect it (VT-58–VT-60) |
| SmartPI (external proportional algorithm, vtherm_smartpi 0.4.0) | PI with feed-forward, learns a room model and its own outdoor term (`u_ff1`) | Pauses and resumes its learning with `set_smartpi_learning` during hot water, foreign heat and water swings, read back from `smartpi_learning_enabled` (VT-61–VT-67); its further signals later (VT-68) |
| Other external algorithms (e.g. vtherm_hysteresis) | Their own regulation | Treated as any zone through VT's published data; no pause |
| Central configuration entry | Shared settings, central boiler, power | Read only: its `thermostat_type`, the central-boiler flag, commands, activation delay and keep-alive (VT-49–VT-56) |
| Central mode select (Auto, Stopped, Heat only, Cool only, Frost protection) | Applies a mode to thermostats that follow it | Read for diagnostics; acts only through the zones' demand; "Stopped" is not a hand-back (VT-46, VT-47) |
| Central boiler (binary sensor, count and power thresholds, activation delay, keep-alive, on/off actions) | Switches a boiler on/off from device count or power | Replaced by the plugin: while configured (or seen configured in this run) control is blocked; its delay carried over; its relay, repeat interval and thresholds offered as a pre-fill (VT-49–VT-57) |
| `is_used_by_central_boiler` per thermostat | Includes the room in VT's boiler count | Read only for the Auto-TPI issue and the count pre-fill; the plugin's own zones are picked in its form |
| Power management (device power, mean cycle power, units, shedding) | Sheds rooms above a house power limit | Power criterion from VT's mean cycle power; shedding removes the zone's demand (VT-25, VT-26, VT-36) |
| Safety mode (`safety_state`, safety duty cycle) | Runs a room on a fixed duty when its sensor goes quiet | Zone flagged as a lost sensor (alarm); its demand still follows VT (VT-27) |
| Window, motion, presence, timed preset, lock, humidity, auto-start/stop | Change mode or target | Seen only through the zone's mode, target and action |
| Heating failure detection | VT's own room alarm | Not used (related plugin, no overlap) |
| `max_on_percent` | Caps the duty cycle | Read as the zone's "fully open" limit (VT-24) |
| `is_ready`, start at `EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_STARTED`, placeholder "off" | Starts thermostats once Home Assistant runs | Recognition period, grace, `ha_starting` (VT-22, VT-40–VT-43) |
| Feature-manager API (vtherm_api ≥ 0.4, VT ≥ 10.2.0) | Creates outside managers per thermostat | Registers a publish-only manager: attribute `smart_boiler`, properties `heat_available`, `emitter_power_factor` (VT-01–VT-14) |
| Reload (service and entry reloads; API dropped with the last entry) | Rebuilds thermostats | Recognition period or per-zone grace; registers again on a new API; asks for VT's reload where a thermostat started before the registration (VT-03, VT-13, VT-41, VT-42) |
| Thermostat entry data (`temperature_sensor_entity_id`, `underlying_entity_ids`) | VT's configuration | Read for the lost-sensor flag, the zone-on-boiler-thermostat and relay checks (VT-28, VT-29) |
| VT services (set_presence, set_safety, set_window_bypass, set_auto_regulation_mode, set_auto_fan_mode, set_hvac_mode_sleep, lock, unlock, set_tpi_parameters, set_auto_tpi_mode, auto_tpi_calibrate_capacity, set/cancel_timed_preset, recalibrate_valves, download_logs, reload) | User and automation actions | None is called (VT-54) |
| Events (central boiler, power, safety, …) | Notify automations | Not used; a "heating commanded" entity and event later (VT-74) |
| Recorder exclusions (`specific_states`, `configuration`, manager dicts) | Keeps nested attributes out of history | Nested data read live only; history from top-level keys (VT-31) |
| Units (°F, W/kW) | Per installation | Converted to °C and kW (VT-18, VT-25) |

### C.2 Checked by the VT task and found sound

- Every VT 10.4.0 key the plugin reads exists where it reads it: climate attributes
  (`hvac_action`, `on_percent`, `valve_open_percent`, `is_ready`, `specific_states.is_device_active`,
  `.last_temperature_datetime`, `configuration.max_on_percent`, `.proportional_function`,
  `.is_used_by_central_boiler`, `power_manager.*`, `safety_manager.safety_state`), entry keys
  (`thermostat_type`, `use_central_boiler_feature`, the two commands, the activation delay,
  `keep_alive_boiler_delay_sec`, `temperature_sensor_entity_id`, `underlying_entity_ids`), unique IDs
  (`central_boiler_state`, `central_mode`, `boiler_activation_threshold`,
  `boiler_power_activation_threshold`); SmartPI's service, field and flag; vtherm_api 0.5.0's
  registry methods and the InterfaceFeatureManager contract.
- VT calls `add_custom_attributes`, `post_init`, `start_listening`, `stop_listening` and
  `restore_state` without a guard; the plugin's methods cannot raise there.
- Home Assistant 2026.9.3 adds VT to `config.components` before VT's entries are set up, so the
  registration made on VT's first climate state comes before the thermostat's `async_startup` retry.
- VT's activation delay and Auto-TPI's "central boiler off" skip behave as the plugin assumes
  (VT: feature_central_boiler_manager.py:139-210; auto_tpi_manager.py:768-770, 2701-2702).

### C.3 Not checked by the VT task

- VT versions other than 10.4.0 (the plugin's capability detection was read, not run against them).
- A real Home Assistant start or a real reload of all VT entries with control running (only the
  in-process tests, which use VT's observed states).

## Appendix D. Untested paths

From the tests-coverage task (`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/tests-coverage-notes.md`), which read
every missing line and arc of the 58 modules from the Tools pass, classified them by safety
relevance, ran 56 hand-picked mutants of safety decisions against the suite, probed untested writer
code, and scanned every integration test under four forced orders of timers due together. Its work
ran on two copies of the tracked code made with `git archive` under
`.tmp/analysis-2026-10-04/tests-coverage/` (diffed identical to the package afterwards). Sections
D.3–D.8 are copied from its sections 3–8 and keep their numbers, so a cross-reference such as
"section 4" in the text reads D.4. Its "finding n" labels are the task's own: 1 → TB-01; 2
and 3 → PB-25; 4 → PB-22; 5 → TB-05; 6 → TB-06; 7 → TB-02 and TB-03; 8 → TB-07; 9 → TB-13;
10 → TB-30; 11 → TB-04; 12 → TB-31; 13 → TB-12; 14 → TB-03; 15 → PB-59; 16 → TB-28.

### D.3 Untested branches in safety-relevant code

Coverage is honest: no `pragma: no cover` anywhere in the package and no `exclude_lines`
configured, so every line counts. Figures as the Tools agent reported them (both runs together:
lines 98.95 %, branches 97.11 %, combined 98.51 %; 135 lines and 116 arcs missing).

Legend — Risk: what a regression in that path could cause in a house, if the path were broken
and no test noticed. "Guard" = defensive code for a case the design says cannot occur.

### D.4 Mutation probes: do the tests notice a changed safety decision?

56 mutants (`mutants.py` 42, `mutants2.py` 12, `mutants3.py` 2); a mutant is "killed" when at
least one test fails. With `-n 3 -x` pytest exits 2 ("interrupted") after the first failure;
those are kills too (the failing test is recorded). No kill was by the order-dependent test of
section 6.

Killed (51), with the first test that failed (FR2 below the table):

| Id | Decision changed | Killed by |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | circuit maximum no longer caps the flow | test_limits.py::test_the_lowest_cap_wins |
| L2 | hard minimum wins over an installation cap | test_limits.py::test_caps_that_protect_the_installation_win_over_the_hard_minimum |
| L3 | a lowered cap ramped instead of applied at once (S-23) | test_controller.py::test_a_lowered_cap_applies_at_once |
| L4 | fallback without outdoor temperature = hard minimum | test_controller.py::test_missing_outdoor_temperature_uses_the_fallback_setpoint |
| F1 | frost heating without hysteresis | test_limits.py::test_frost_starts_below_the_limit_and_releases_with_hysteresis |
| F2 | no frost heat for a zone whose valve state is unknown | same |
| F3 | lost link never hands back | test_controller.py::test_a_read_back_lost_while_controlling_is_no_hand_back_by_itself |
| G1 | another controller rewritten every time | test_guards.py (second fall-back within an hour) |
| G2 | another value judged after one step | test_guards.py::test_a_heating_echo_unknown_at_the_first_send_still_tells_a_fall_back |
| G3 | own expiring lapse never recognised | test_guards.py::test_a_lapse_after_our_own_silence_is_resent_not_rewritten |
| G4 | expiring override repeated every 60 s | test_guards.py::test_first_write_and_expiring_keepalive |
| R1 | 30 untraced relay restarts answered a day | test_relay.py::test_a_fourth_power_cut_state_restart_in_a_day_steps_aside |
| R2 | shortest recognised relay timer 1 min (K4.2) | test_relay.py::test_whole_multiples_count_only_for_a_timer_of_ten_minutes_or_more |
| R4 | relay in the wrong state never corrected by the check | test_relay.py::test_an_unconfirmed_command_is_reported_and_clears_when_it_holds |
| R5 | relay hand-back always "on" | test_writers.py::test_relay_writer_switch |
| H1 | entity hand-back never turns heating back on | test_writers.py::test_entity_writer_with_value_hand_back |
| H2 | opentherm_gw hand-back sends CH=0 | test_writers.py::test_opentherm_gw_writer |
| H3 | MQTT hand-back sends chenable 0 | test_writers.py::test_mqtt_writer |
| H4 | held value target always released | test_hand_back.py (held target released only at the value) |
| H5 | read-back at the lowest counts as release | test_hand_back.py (expiring target) |
| H6 | timeout hand-back rewritten every retry | test_control.py::test_a_timeout_hand_back_is_confirmed_when_the_read_back_returns_to_the_baseline |
| H7 | debt not stored before the first hand-back write (P-42) | test_control.py::test_a_real_cancel_of_a_hand_back_keeps_it_owed_and_stored |
| H8 | failed hand-back retried every 10 min | test_control.py::test_a_hand_back_cut_by_a_foreign_cancel_stays_owed |
| H10 | no persistent issue when unloading with a debt | test_control.py::test_disabling_the_entry_with_a_hand_back_owed_keeps_the_issue |
| H11 | unload does not stop the units | test_acceptance.py::test_hand_back_on_every_exit[unload] (not test_setup.py) |
| S1 | stored "controlling" no longer makes the hand-back owed | test_control.py::test_a_setup_that_fails_late_leaves_nothing_running |
| S2 | holding flag not saved before a write | test_control.py::test_the_one_rewrite_and_the_latch_are_stored_at_once |
| S3 | restore through changed options | test_control.py::test_a_restore_whose_conditions_fail_hands_back_first[no_options_stored] |
| S4 | restore with the switch entity disabled | same [switch_disabled] |
| S5 | stored flag set only when exactly True | test_const.py::test_owes_hand_back_reads_flags_cautiously |
| S6 | unreadable control state never owes | test_const.py::test_an_unreadable_state_is_owed_with_control_configured |
| S7 | downgrade drops the control store's debt | test_control.py::test_a_store_written_by_0_2_1_after_a_downgrade_keeps_a_hand_back_owed[store] |
| B1 | control during the monitoring period | test_control.py::test_control_is_refused_during_monitoring |
| B2 | control beside VT's active central boiler | test_control.py::test_vt_central_boiler_blocks_control |
| B3 | VT's central boiler unknown: grace for ever | test_control.py::test_a_vt_central_boiler_unknown_does_not_hand_back_within_the_grace |

| C3 | gateway taken before its read-back has a value (P-21) | test_control.py::test_otgw_control_waits_until_the_gateway_has_reported |
| C4 | opentherm_gw entry removed does not block | test_control.py::test_a_gateway_entry_removed_or_disabled_blocks_control |
| C5 | external switch not kept alive | test_control.py::test_an_expiring_external_switch_is_turned_on_every_keep_alive |
| C6 | external switch off: fight instead of step aside | test_control.py::test_the_external_control_switch_switched_off_steps_aside_without_a_rewrite |
| W2 | write to an unavailable entity attempted | test_writers.py::test_a_missing_or_unavailable_target_is_a_failure |
| W3 | external switch not turned on before a setpoint | test_writers.py::test_entity_writer_switch_and_timeout_hand_back |
| W4 | expiring external switch renewed every 60 s | test_writers.py::test_an_expiring_external_switch_is_due_again_every_keep_alive |
| W5 | entity range check removed | test_writers.py::test_a_value_off_the_entitys_grid_or_range_is_refused |

FR2 (water path: frost heating's start reset at every step) — killed by
test_controller.py::test_frost_heating_that_does_not_warm_the_zone_is_reported_not_stopped.

Survived the whole suite (5):

- **FR1** (`core/controller.py:923`, the relay path's `_on_off_decision`: frost heating's start
  reset at every step, so `frost_stuck` — the alarm "frost heating does not warm the room" — can
  never rise on an on/off boiler): no test fails, core or Home Assistant. The arc 925->927 (frost
  heating continuing past its first step on the relay path) is never taken: the only relay frost
  test, `test_on_off_mode_idle_and_frost` (test_controller.py:2009-2017), runs one step, and no
  integration test runs frost protection on the relay path. The same mutant on the water path
  (FR2) is caught. On a relay installation without a proof input mapped, "boiler not
  responding" (R12) stays "unverified", so this alarm is the only one that tells of frost
  heating that does not heat — a regression would leave a freezing house without an alarm.
  High (missing test).
- **R3** (`core/relay.py:634`, `_renew_s` for a recognised timer returns the configured renewal
  instead of `min(seen / 2, repeat_s)`): nearly an equivalent mutant since K4.2 — with
  `TIMER_RECOGNISED_S` = 540 s, `seen / 2` ≥ 270 s and `repeat_s` ≤ 300 s, so the two differ
  only for a recognised 9-10 min timer with a repeat interval above `seen / 2`, by at most
  30 s. Z4R2-05's rule is now unpinned and almost dead. Low.
- **H9** (`control.py:1302-1303`, the stop's TimeoutError no longer keeps the hand-back owed):
  the only test that reaches the timeout (`test_the_stop_hand_back_fits_home_assistants_budget
  [budget_cut]`, test_control.py:5551-5627) times out during the writes, when
  `_async_try_hand_back` has already set and stored the debt, so the line is redundant there.
  It matters when the budget runs out before the hand-back starts (the lock held, e.g. by
  `async_set_enabled` awaiting a save): then it is the only thing that raises the persistent
  "hand-back owed" issue at that stop — for a disabled entry, the only alarm (the stored
  `controlling` flag covers a later start, not a disabled entry). Medium.
- **W1** (`writers.py:600`, an opentherm_gw setpoint write no longer counts as failed while the
  gateway's read-back entity is unavailable): no test writes a setpoint (as opposed to a
  hand-back, which is tested at test_control.py:2415-2460) to a disconnected gateway and checks
  "write failed" and the resend. Low (the link-lost rule and the guards' trace still act).
- **W6** (`writers.py:187-188`, `_finite` removed): the line is never executed (section 3). Low.

What this shows: the safety decisions of the core (limits, frost, link, guards, relay, demand,
recognition) and of control's lifecycle (stores, restore, blockers, hand-back writes and
judgement) are not only covered but asserted. The gaps are in defensive paths, and in the relay
path's frost alarm (FR1).

### D.5 Behaviour probes of untested writer code

`probe/tests/integration/test_probe_tc_writers.py` (direct `EntityWriter`) and
`test_probe_tc_clamp.py` (end to end, the `rig` of test_control.py); outputs in
`runs/probe-own-control-clamp.txt` and `runs/probe-clamp-e2e.txt`.

- writers.py:186-189 `_finite`: `WriteError("setpoint … outside 0 to 90")` for NaN, inf, -1 and
  95 on an entity without min/max/step; no call made. Holds.
- writers.py:445-454: the hand-back value goes through `grid.put(value, None, highest)` when the
  setpoint entity has a step. `Grid.put` → `on_grid` returns "the one just inside where the
  nearest falls outside" (`core/limits.py:115-139`, `Grid.put` 183-199), so a hand-back value outside the entity's
  current min/max (or above the highest water temperature) is replaced by the nearest bound and
  the check expects the replaced value. Without a step the same value is refused
  (`_as_entity_takes_it`: "0 outside its range" → `HandBackFailed`, owed, alarm). Direct probe,
  hand-back value 0 ("own control"), min 10: with step 1 → writes 20 (lowest), CH on, then
  **10.0**, check `expected=10.0`; without step → `FAILED number.boiler_flow: 0 outside its
  range`.
- End to end (held number with step 1, min 0 at the take, heating switch held, hand-back value
  0 declared "the boiler's own control resumes"): control heats (38 °C); the entity's minimum
  rises to 10; next step: blocker `setpoint_outside_entity_range`, hand-back writes 25 (the
  lowest in that rig), heating switch on, then **10.0**; read back 10 → hand-back confirmed,
  `hand_back_owed` False, `alarm_hand_back_failed` off, no `hand_back_owed`,
  `control_stopped_heating` or `hand_back_taken_by_other` issue; ten minutes on nothing changes.
  The boiler keeps a 10 °C water setpoint with heating enabled, and the value that would have
  given it its own control back (0) was never sent. `_stops_heating()` is false for an "own
  control" value (`control_config.py:837-851`), so neither S-10's issue nor "handed back in
  frost" can rise. S-21 (SCOPE.md:69-70, 214-215) bounds the hand-back value; the S-21 test says
  "It is never clamped" (test_control.py:6340-6344). Precondition: the entity's range changes
  while control holds the boiler (the blocker refuses such a value at the take).

### D.6 A test whose outcome depends on timer order

- Baseline 1 (whole non-core suite in the copy, `-n 3`): 1 failed —
  `tests/integration/test_acceptance.py::test_an_undeclared_relay_timer_is_recognised_and_heating_goes_on`,
  `assert found is not None` at line 1681 (the `relay_timer_seen` issue missing; control
  heating, relay on, outside_change off). Baseline 2 (whole suite, `-n 3`): 3128 passed.
  Alone: 5 + 8 (under the load of another run) passes.
- Mechanism, verified: the harness fires timer handles due together in the heap's array order
  (`common.py:543`, `async_.py:136`), which depends on what else is scheduled on the real loop
  clock. The simulator's 10-s refresh (`boiler_sim/__init__.py:180`) and the plugin's 10-s
  control step are due together. Forcing the plugin's step first
  (`probe/tests/integration/test_probe_tc_order.py`, `runs/probe-timer-order.txt`) fails the
  test three times out of three; the simulator first passes three out of three. With the
  plugin first, the 5-min "on" renewal (due every 300 s; 30 min is six of them) reaches the
  relay just after its own 30-min timer switched it off, restarts the timer and hides every
  lapse, so the timer is never recognised. The sibling test uses 27 min "so the 5-min renewals
  do not happen to hide its lapses" (test_acceptance.py:1690-1692); this one does not.
- CI runs the suite sequentially; the heap order still depends on real timing there, so the
  test can fail in CI. Heating is unaffected in either order (each lapse is answered at once);
  what flips is the repair issue asking to declare the timer.
- Order scan (every integration test under four forced orders): see 6a.

#### D.6a Order scan

Every integration test (1182) in the probe copy with an autouse fixture added to its conftest
that forces the order of due handles (`PROBE_ORDER`): `plugin_first`, `sim_first`, `reversed`
(the heap array backwards), `chronological` (sorted by due time). Outputs `runs/order-*.txt`.
`test_boiler_sim.py::test_the_test_ha_configuration_is_valid` fails in all four only because the
probe copy has no `devenv/` (an artifact; it reads `devenv/config/configuration.yaml`).

| Order | Failures besides the artifact |
|---|---|
| chronological | none |
| sim_first | none |
| plugin_first | test_acceptance.py::test_an_undeclared_relay_timer_is_recognised_and_heating_goes_on |
| reversed | the same, and test_control.py::test_learning_is_paused_during_hot_water_and_released_on_switch_off |

The second one: after `rig.advance(10)` the SmartPI pause was called but the control-state
sensor's `learning_paused` is still `[]` (test_control.py:1097-1098). The control step runs as a
background task (`_TrackTimeInterval._interval_listener` → `async_run_hass_job(...,
background=True)`, event.py:1620), and the rigs' `advance` waits with
`async_block_till_done()` (test_control.py:244-251, test_acceptance.py:152-159), which does not
wait for background tasks; the status is published by `_notify()` after the SmartPI calls
(control.py:1357-1358). With `async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)` in the copy's
`advance`, the test passes under the reversed order (checked, then the file restored). The same
change does not cure the relay-timer test (it still fails with the plugin first: a behaviour
difference, not a wait).

So two tests of about 1180 depend on the firing order; the rest pass under all four orders.

### D.7 What holds

- Coverage is measured as CI measures it, with branches, without exclusions; every module is
  at or above its floor; config_flow.py is at 100 % (both flows).
- The three xfails are `strict=True` (tests/sim/test_control_loop.py:336-337,
  tests/test_release.py:118): a fix would turn them red, not pass silently.
- Read-only by construction is pinned by a spy on `hass.services.async_call` itself
  (test_no_writes.py), including calls to unregistered services.
- 51 of 56 mutants of safety decisions are caught; every core mutant but FR1 is caught by the
  core tests alone (fast, `-p no:homeassistant`).
- The untested writer guard `_finite` works as written (probe).
- `async_read_control_state`, `restore()`, `stored()` and the cautious flag readings are fully
  covered and asserted (S1-S7).

### D.8 Not done, assumed

- The ha-minimum leg (Home Assistant 2026.9.0) and the latest-vtherm-api job were not run;
  `__init__.py` sits exactly at 95.00 %, so one more uncovered line or arc there — or a branch
  that goes the other way on 2026.9.0 — turns CI red. I found no Home Assistant version check in
  `__init__.py`, `forecasts.py` or `coordinator.py` (assumed: same coverage on 2026.9.0).
- The mutants are hand-picked, not exhaustive; a killed mutant shows a test notices that
  change, not every change.
- Whether a real device's number entity changes its min/max while in use (the precondition of
  section 5) was not researched.

## Appendix E. Open questions for the user

Decisions the review cannot make; each points to the problem it would settle. Copied from
`research/2026-10-04-review-raw/problems.md`.

1. **SB-02 (decision 3 against VT's placeholder):** should a VT thermostat that VT has not
   started (is_ready false) count as unknown after the recognition period — so the plugin hands
   back to a working thermostat or raises "no zone known" — instead of as the user's "off" (S-34)?
2. **SB-01:** add a "no sign the boiler heats" information alarm and repair issue on setpoint
   paths (as R12 on the relay path), or keep it a known limit stated in K1's README?
3. **PB-03:** when no configured demand criterion can be judged, which rule wins — SCOPE §7 (the
   "no zone known" alarm and a repair issue) or plan X3 (the criterion alarm only)?
4. **SB-03:** a heating "on" ignored from the start — keep writing "off" (stop only the state not
   taken), or block and hand back as answer O does for "off"; and raise the "house not heated"
   issue whatever the hand-back effect?
5. **SB-04:** may the timeout hand-back count as released once the read-back has left both the
   plugin's value and the lowest, and should the form ask the device's timeout?
6. **SB-05, SB-06 (relays):** bound a declared timer's lapse to ±60 s of its length (multiples up
   to 3×)? A relay that stops taking commands mid-session: an error issue and a block for "off",
   decided together with #16 and #33?
7. **SB-07:** pre-fill a count threshold of 0 at migration where VT used power alone?
8. **SB-08:** J4's starts criterion — the plugin's curve equal to the boiler's own, or a
   comfort-parity check, and with the ±3 K daily swing; which ratio at −5 °C is acceptable given
   #27's ×2.16 with the defaults?
9. **PB-08:** adopt the cautious latch rule (VT's central entry changed in this run before any
   plugin entry watched VT → wait for the restart)?
10. **SB-36:** exclude the external-switch hand-back from the optional return by itself, as relays
    are?
11. **SB-11:** build the comfort correction's extreme-weather freeze (and define "extreme") before
    the option stays offered, or record that the correction does not meet principle 13's rules 3
    and 5?
12. **SB-10:** raise a persistent error issue when a setup fails where a hand-back stops heating?
13. **SB-18:** the high-pressure alarm — no default (as "add water"), or one below the lowest
    common relief rating?
14. **SB-29:** should a boiler-fault "off" pause zone learning as a hot-water draw does?
15. **Documents:** the "Open after" additions of SB-09 may be committed at once under the rules;
    the other SB fixes (SCOPE, the plans, the details, devenv/README.md with the Z3 proposal) need
    a shown diff and consent — apply them before K4?

## Appendix F. Findings refuted during verification

Nine findings did not survive their adversarial verifier; they are in none of the tables of
sections 3, 4 and 6. One line each: the task, the finding's title, and why it was refuted (from the
verifiers' reasons; the sources they cite were checked for this report).

| # | Task | Finding | Why refuted |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | P21 | Monitor-only OTGW firmware over MQTT: the 0 bar after a PIC reset raises "add water" | A recorded, accepted limit: `docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:3672` (Y1, "Missing data") says that with the OTGW firmware over MQTT and monitor only (or another write path) 0.0 bar counts as a reading, since no `otgw_mqtt` read-back names the gateway's device, and that a wrong add-water notification after a gateway reset only informs. Matrix row P21-120 still shows "partial" for it. |
| 2 | P21 | "last_change" moves on resends and rewrites of the same value | As documented and specified: `ControlStatus.last_change_at` is "last write of a new value (keep-alives aside)" (`control.py:622`), and O3 (`docs/plan-0.2.1.md:261`) asks only that keep-alives do not move it. |
| 3 | HA | Two repair issues only inform: `monitor_recovered` and `reactions_removed` | Deliberate notices: `monitor_recovered` implements the user's answer I of 2026-09-27, "with an information note" (`docs/plan-0.2.2.md:204, 348, 497`), and the details chose a repair issue for it on purpose, Home Assistant having no information level for issues (`docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:1847-1850`); `reactions_removed` is Y1's one warning naming the dropped reactions (`docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:3608`). Matrix row HA-50 still shows "partial" for it. |
| 4 | transport | A switch hand-back through an `assumed_state` external switch stays owed while the setpoint entity is unavailable, though the switch went off | The behaviour is real (probe: with the setpoint number unavailable, the external-switch check is ASSUMED with `written=False`), but it is what the specification asks: `SCOPE.md` §5 (lines 204-207) — a missing or unavailable target means the hand-back failed, and an `assumed_state` entity is "unconfirmed" (S-09). |
| 5 | entities-texts | The Features count mixes "not set up" with "not working now" | The specified design: Y4 rule 20 (`docs/plan-0.2.2-details.md:3986-3990`) makes the features sensor's state the number of inactive features, and the user's missing-data rule (`SCOPE.md:476`) counts a feature whose input is missing, unknown or unavailable as inactive. |
| 6 | vt-model | Frost protection still uses the frozen temperature of a zone whose room sensor VT has lost | The code fact is true (`zone_max_age_s` is None, `core/controller.py:227-231`: a steady reading is not a stale one, and a room sensor gone quiet is VT's own safety mode's matter), but the behaviour is already decided, and the finding misstated which alarms cover the case. |
| 7 | spec-document | The "clipped" class almost never fires: a boiler that caps a steady or slowly rising setpoint is judged another controller, and the plugin steps aside | The probe passed un-gridded steps of 0.2–0.4 K straight into `guards.plan_write`; the real loop first puts the setpoint on the entity's grid (`core/loop.py:174-190, 262-265`), so the probe did not show what a real installation sees. |
| 8 | tests-integration | Diagnostics are tested only by calling the function, never through Home Assistant's diagnostics | Neither failure the finding names would pass unnoticed; for platform discovery, Home Assistant imports the integration's `diagnostics` module and reads `async_get_config_entry_diagnostics` from it by name. |
| 9 | tests-sim | `devenv/README.md` still gives the pre-Z3 J4 setup; the pending consent to Z3's text is on no open list | The stale sections are expected — the replacement waits for consent under CLAUDE.md's rule for documents — and the pending text is tracked: `research/2026-09-27-k4-questions.md:175-178` names the proposal (`research/2026-10-02-z3-readme-proposal.md`), "not applied; waits for consent at J4". The stale guide itself stays as SB-23 (low; found by PL and tooling-ci-sim). |
